| H2 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | Strike disposal runs on the Metal flusher via `MTLGraphicsConfig.getContext()` while the Skia flusher dereferences `GlyphInfo` | `getContext()` returns the `MTLContext` (`MTLGraphicsConfig.java:168`) — confirmed statically; race window not reproduced | real, latent |
| H3 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | Lock-order inversion MTL→SK (`MTLLayer.drawInMTLContext`) vs SK→MTL (texture-paint proxy creation inside `validate`), plus nested `validateContext` mid-validation | lock order confirmed (`MTLLayer.java:148-163`); `SKPaints.isTextureValid` indeed lacks `MTLPaints`' out-of-lock proxy pre-caching | real; deadlock needs the display callback to coincide, mis-targeted primitive not yet reproduced |
| H4 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | Texture paint reads every source as `INT_ARGB_PRE` (`SKBackend.mm:690`) | confirmed statically | real for INT_ARGB with α<255 and INT_RGB sources until the proxy texture takes over |
| H5 ✔ `b864d1becb49` (delegate kept reachable until the Skia side disposed) | `SKSDOps.mtl` may dangle between the two Disposer records; `IsoBlit` adds no source reference | not verified | plausible |
| M1 ✔ `978bd2a8ea3a` (confirmed at 1×: 67 XOR pixels) | Polyline joints drawn twice (`DrawPoly` segments inclusive) | not verified; visible under XOR/translucent only | plausible |
| M2 ✔ `b864d1becb49` | `SYNC` opcode does not wait for the GPU (`EndOfFlush` → `Submit(cc, false)`) | confirmed (`SKRenderQueue.mm:286`, `SKBackend.mm:1525`) | real; `Toolkit.sync()` semantics |
| M3 ✔ `978bd2a8ea3a` | Cross-context images wrapped with the source's recorder | not verified; multi-display only | plausible |
| M4 ✔ `dced1a0e45d9` | Second Graphite context created at startup for the description string | known (§13 item) | confirmed |
| M5 ✔ `978bd2a8ea3a` | AA/LCD glyph batches and no-cache glyph draws can reorder within a list | not verified | plausible |

All LOW findings fixed in `dced1a0e45d9`/`978bd2a8ea3a` except L4 (the Metal headers pull in Cocoa/AWTWindow/MTLLayer; the mirror stays, documented). **Optimizations:** 1 (texture-paint rule) with H3; 2 zero-copy uploads `38c2b7ed344e`; 4–6 one quad batch `dd86fb1c0caa`; 3 (persistent upload surface) **not done** — the only software-upload tests (`ArgbSwBlitImage`, `BgrSwBlitImage`, 12.6 FPS on both pipelines) are bound by the Java-side image generation, not the upload; 7 (shape clip as `SkRegion`) **not done** — Graphite's `clipRegion` converts the region to a path, nothing to gain; 8 (`drawEdgeAAImageSet`, knob `db78d3f555ff`) **measured, not adopted** — same speed, ~10k matrix pixels differ by a few levels, experimental API; `OPTIMIZATION := HIGH` **measured, kept LOW** — 1.00× over 19 tests (the interpreter is not where the time goes).

Also listed: 8 low findings (stale comments, trace levels, interop header mirroring, build hygiene, `unpremul` rounding vs `div8table`, UB on extreme blit scales), a consistency table (two items Metal should adopt: clipped/chunked software blits and `SwToSurfaceTransform` for all int types), and 9 cleanup/optimization items (FILL_RECT into the rect batch, batching non-AA parallelograms/blits, merging the two quad batches, persistent upload surface, zero-copy uploads, `drawEdgeAAQuad` experiment for D1). The agent's verdict matches mine: the core is sound; every HIGH is at the seam with the Metal pipeline (two queues, two disposer records, two locks, Metal-owned `BufferedContext`).

### 11.15 JCEF demo, Metal vs. Skia `shapes=analytic` (2026-10-14)

Setup (`build/skia-smoke/jcef/`): `JcefDemo.java` embeds JCEF 144.0.15 from the IDEA `jcef-plugin` bundle the way the IDE does — off-screen rendering through a custom `CefRenderHandler`, BGRA frames copied into an `INT_ARGB_PRE` image and drawn with `drawImage` (bilinear, 1:1 at 2×) under a Swing toolbar (Aqua button, checkbox, progress bar, combo box). Two instances of the jbr25 image side by side, Metal and Skia with `sun.java2d.skia.shapes=analytic`, page `page.html` (text, CSS rounded buttons, SVG gradient rrect/circles/triangle/lines/dashes). Captured by window id (`winid.m` + `screencapture -l`), diffed with `DiffImage` (max channel difference per pixel).

| Region (device px, 1400 wide) | Differing | > 32 levels | Max |
|---|---|---|---|
| Browser content (CEF bitmap blit, 1400×1260) | **0** | 0 | 0 |
| Swing toolbar (1400×84) | 2772 (2.4 %) | 0 | 31 |

- The browser content is **pixel-identical**: JCEF rasterizes in its own process; the Java2D side only blits the bitmap, and the blit path is exact on both pipelines. JCEF therefore says nothing about shape rasterization (as the brief predicted: JCEF synergy is OS-level texture interop, not rendering).
- Every remaining difference is **text in the Swing labels** (`JCEF demo`, `Reload`, `Rounded controls`, `Combo`), ≤ 31 levels on glyph edge pixels — the known LCD-text blending difference (Metal shader vs. Skia LCD blender, §11.13). Aqua controls (button, checkbox, progress bar, combo) are identical.
- Pitfalls for anyone repeating this: (1) JCEF must be started via `JCefAppConfig.getInstance(bundlePath)` → `CefApp.startup(args)` → `CefApp.getInstance(args, settings, null)` with `CefApp.setIsRemoteEnabled(false)`, otherwise CEF never initializes (no helper processes, no `onPaint`); (2) two instances need distinct `settings.cache_path` (Chromium's process singleton aborts the second); (3) the embedded Chromium triggers a screen-centred macOS Apple-Events permission dialog — place the windows at the screen edges; (4) **window activation changes Aqua's rendering** (button border, accent colours): compare only with both windows inactive and settled, otherwise the diff reports 50–220-level differences that are not rendering.

### 11.16 Paint cost inside the IDE, method timing (2026-10-15, n = 3 per arm)

JDK 25 method timing (exact invocation counts and summed wall time, no sampling) on the `GeometryScenesPerformanceTest` run (java-design-patterns project, ≈ 190 s per launch: 20 distraction-free toggles, 20 hide/restore-all-tool-windows, 8 Settings open/close, 15 Search Everywhere open/close, 22 tabs × 100 NextTab, 400 scroll steps). Same IDE build (271.596 nightly), same jbr25 Skia image, only the pipeline property differs; async profiler off.

| Method (sum over the run) | Metal | Skia + Marlin | Skia analytic |
|---|---|---|---|
| `RepaintManager.paintDirtyRegions` — Swing paint on the EDT, incl. queue flushes | **15.2 s** (5264 × 2.88 ms) | 11.5 s (4990 × 2.31 ms) | **10.2 s** (4978 × 2.04 ms) |
| render queue `flushNow` (Java caller of the native flush) | 7.3 s (6767 × 1.08 ms) | 4.1 s (6437 × 0.64 ms) | 3.4 s (6380 × 0.54 ms) |
| `EventQueue.dispatchEvent` total | 33.8 s | 29.3 s | 28.4 s |
| `AAShapePipe.renderTiles` (Marlin tiles → mask fills) | 0.95 s (33 850 shapes) | 0.94 s (33 466) | 0.39 s (15 644) |
| `DMarlinRenderingEngine.getAATileGenerator` | 0.58 s (64 358) | 0.58 s (61 694) | 0.35 s (42 703) |
| `BufferedMaskFill.MaskFill` (per tile, EDT encode only) | 0.19 s (598 553 tiles) | 0.19 s (602 241) | 0.06 s (124 655) |
| `SKShapePipe.render` (analytic shapes) | — | 0 | 0.06 s (18 268 shapes) |

**Three repetitions per arm (medians, [min..max], seconds summed per ≈ 190 s run):**

| Method | Metal | Skia + Marlin | Skia analytic |
|---|---|---|---|
| `EventQueue.dispatchEvent` | 33.45 [33.39..33.83] | 29.29 [29.17..29.57] | 28.43 [28.13..28.73] |
| `RepaintManager.paintDirtyRegions` | **15.06** [14.94..15.16] | 11.46 [11.20..11.53] | **10.24** [10.16..10.32] |
| render queue `flushNow` | 7.31 [7.24..7.38] | 3.85 [3.78..4.09] | 3.49 [3.41..3.54] |
| `AAShapePipe.renderTiles` | 0.87 [0.86..0.95] | 0.93 [0.90..0.94] | 0.40 [0.39..0.43] |
| `BufferedMaskFill.MaskFill` | 0.17 [0.15..0.18] | 0.18 [0.17..0.19] | 0.06 [0.05..0.06] |
| `SKShapePipe.render` | — | 0 | 0.06 [0.05..0.06] |

Run-to-run spread is ≤ 2 % on every row, so the differences between arms (paint −24 % Skia+Marlin, −32 % Skia analytic vs. Metal; flush −47 % / −52 %) are far outside noise. The first-repetition reading below stands.

Reading: (1) the analytic mode takes 18 268 of ≈ 34 000 AA shapes and 79 % of the Marlin mask tiles out of the frame; Marlin's own CPU work for them was only ≈ 0.7 s of the 15 s paint time, so the direct rasterization saving is small — **the saving shows up in the flush**: 7.3 s → 3.4 s, i.e. fewer, smaller tile uploads and fewer draws per flush. (2) Swing paint time drops by a third against Metal (15.2 → 10.2 s) and EDT dispatch by 16 %; Skia with Marlin tiles already gets most of it (11.5 s), the analytic shapes add the rest. (3) Scene wall-clock times were equal across arms because the scenes are paced by fixed waits, so this is EDT headroom, not visible frame rate — consistent with the latency parity measured before. (4) Metal's `flushNow` average of 1.08 ms vs Skia's 0.54–0.64 ms was first attributed to the Metal flusher's per-op command-buffer commits (`MTLRenderQueue.m` commits after every `RESET_PAINT` and every mask-op run). **Measured and refuted the same evening** (arm `metal-coalesced`, `sun.java2d.metal.coalesceCommits=true`, SrcOver colour/gradient paints only, pixel-identical matrix): coalescing made Metal *slower* — flush 8.8 s [8.4..10.4] vs 7.3 s, paint 16.8 s vs 15.2 s, EDT 34.6 s vs 33.5 s (n = 3 vs 5, geometry scenes). The frequent commits pipeline GPU execution with CPU encoding and let the texture pool recycle the 40 KB mask textures from completed buffers; one buffer per flush serializes the two and forces fresh allocations. So Metal's extra flush time has another cause (candidates: per-tile `replaceRegion` uploads of Marlin masks versus Skia's staged atlas rows, the encoder restart per op change, the display-sync hand-off in `MTLLayer`); a native CPU profile of the flusher thread (`sample`/Instruments during a geometry run) is the next step, not a code change. Skia's `flushNow` is not blocked by anything comparable: Graphite submits once and returns.

(5) **Native profile of the Metal flusher** (`sample`, 20 s at 1 ms, during the `typingInJavaFile` repetition 3 run, `build/skia-bench/ijperf/profile-metal-1620.txt`): 7956 samples, 6622 waiting for work, **1334 active**; of the active ones **630 (47 %) in `-[MTLGlyphCache free]`**, almost all in `commitCommandBuffer` → `waitUntilCompleted`, plus 72 in `glyphCacheInitWidth` (texture reallocation through IOGPU/`mach_msg`) and 97 in `replaceRegion` of which 91 in the driver's CPU-side texture compression (`AGX::AppleCompressionGen2`). The Metal glyph cache is 512×512 with 32×32 cells (256 cells per cache, AA and LCD separately); when full, `MTLTR_AddToGlyphCache` **frees the whole cache**: flushes pending glyph quads, commits and waits for the GPU, releases the texture, allocates a new one and re-uploads every glyph afterwards. The Skia backend's atlas (§11.9) is 1024×1024 (1024 cells), evicts per cell through `AccelGlyphCache` and uploads staged rows without a GPU wait — the structural reason for the flush gap in text scenes. The Skia profile window (`profile-skia-analytic-1616.txt`) fell into an idle phase (1 active sample) and is not comparable; re-sample during a typing scene. **Done** (typing-phase windows, 20 s each, `profile-metal-typing-1644.txt` = Kotlin typing, `profile-skia-analytic-typing-1637.txt` = Java typing): Metal flusher 671 active samples, **412 (61 %) in `MTLGlyphCache free` → `waitUntilCompleted`**, 31 in `replaceRegion` (21 compression), 99 in `MTLTR_DrawGlyphList`, 58 in `MTLRenderer_*`, 49 encoder creation; Skia flusher ≈ 500 active samples with no GPU wait at all — 302 in `SKBackend_EndOfFlush` (Graphite `Recorder::snap` 79, `insertRecording`/`Context::submit` 50, mask/glyph atlas flush 52) and 135 in `SkCanvas::*` draw recording. In text scenes the Metal flusher's time is the glyph cache stall, Skia's is recording and submission.

**Final tables (2026-10-15 17:14, all arms from the same jbr25 image, 4–5 repetitions unless noted; seconds summed per run, median [min..max]):**

| Scene | Metric | Metal | Metal coalesced | Skia + Marlin | Skia analytic |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Geometry (n=5) | Swing paint on EDT | 15.2 [14.9..15.7] | 16.8 [15.9..19.0] | 11.4 [11.2..11.5] | **10.3** [10.2..10.6] |
| | EDT dispatch total | 33.5 | 34.6 | 29.3 | 28.5 |
| | render queue flush | 7.3 | 8.8 | 3.9 | 3.5 |
| Typing Java (n=2/4/4/4) | Swing paint | 16.9 [15.8..17.9] | 17.5 | 11.7 | **11.1** [10.8..12.8] |
| | EDT dispatch total | 31.3 | 31.0 | 25.9 | 25.2 |
| Typing Kotlin (n=4) | Swing paint | 15.7 [10.8..17.9] | 13.1 | 9.8 | **7.8** [6.4..10.6] |
| | EDT dispatch total | 27.4 | 24.4 | 20.7 | 18.8 |
| Rendering scenes (n=1/2/2/2) | Swing paint | 11.8 | 13.2 | 9.6 | **7.9** |
| Settings (n=1 per case) | dialog shown, warm | 266 ms | — | 298 ms | 378 ms |

Peak IDE RSS per scene is now within ±3 % across all four arms (e.g. geometry 6.73 / 6.74 / 6.74 / 6.86 GB), except rendering scenes where Skia analytic is +300 MB at n=3; the "+100–300 MB on Skia" reading of the morning does not hold at n ≥ 3. Settings dialog open times are 266 ms (Metal) vs 298/378 ms (Skia) at n = 1 — the one metric where Skia is behind; too few samples to weigh.

**Summary of the day's measurement:** against the shipped Metal pipeline, Skia cuts Swing paint time on the EDT by 25–35 % with Marlin tiles and 32–51 % with analytic shapes, and total EDT dispatch time by 12–31 % depending on how paint-heavy the scene is; typing latency p90 and scene wall-clock are unchanged (scenes are delay-paced, EDT 11–19 % busy). The cause is not Marlin's CPU work (≈ 1 s of 15) and not the per-op command-buffer commits (coalescing them made Metal slower): the Metal flusher stalls on the GPU every time its 256-cell glyph cache fills and is freed wholesale (61 % of its active time in a typing window), plus per-glyph compressed `replaceRegion` uploads; Skia's larger atlas with per-cell eviction and staged uploads never waits. Those are fixable in Metal (§16); after that fix the Skia-vs-Metal gap must be re-measured before any decision rests on it.

### 11.17 Level field: Metal with the glyph-cache fix vs. Skia (2026-10-16, night run, 41/41 runs)

Six arms from one jbr25 image (only system properties differ): `metal` (shipped), `metal-coalesced`, `metal-glyph` (1024² glyph cache, no GPU wait on replacement, uncompressed glyph/mask textures — §12 2026-10-15 evening), `metal-glyph-coalesced`, `skia-marlin`, `skia-analytic`. Seconds summed per run, medians (typing scenes count two launches per repetition):

| Scene | Metric | Metal | Metal coalesced | **Metal glyph** | Metal glyph + coalesced | Skia + Marlin | **Skia analytic** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Geometry (n=5/5/4/4/5/5) | Swing paint | 15.2 | 16.8 | **12.1** [11.7..12.3] | 12.3 | 11.4 | **10.3** [10.2..10.6] |
| | flush | 7.3 | 8.8 | 4.7 | 4.9 | 3.9 | 3.5 |
| | EDT dispatch | 33.5 | 34.6 | 30.6 | 30.3 | 29.3 | 28.5 |
| Typing Java (n=4/8/8/8/6/6) | Swing paint | 16.6 | 17.7 | **13.2** [12.3..13.3] | 13.2 | 12.0 | **11.8** [10.8..12.8] |
| | flush | 8.7 | 9.7 | 4.8 | 5.0 | 4.0 | 3.8 |
| | EDT dispatch | 31.1 | 32.1 | 27.6 | 27.5 | 26.1 | 26.7 |
| Typing Kotlin (n=6/8/8/8/6/6) | Swing paint | 15.7 | 14.9 | **10.5** [6.9..11.3] | 8.8 | 9.9 | **9.1** [6.4..10.6] |
| | flush | 8.7 | 8.5 | 4.1 | 3.7 | 4.0 | 3.4 |
| | EDT dispatch | 27.4 | 26.1 | 22.3 | 19.6 | 20.8 | 20.3 |
| Rendering scenes (n=1/3/3/3/2/2) | Swing paint | 11.8 | 13.4 | **9.5** [9.3..9.5] | 10.3 | 9.6 | **7.9** [7.8..7.9] |
| | flush | 5.9 | 7.4 | 3.8 | 4.4 | 3.3 | 2.7 |

**Reading.**
1. **The glyph-cache stall was the bulk of the Metal-vs-Skia gap.** The three glyph switches cut Metal's flush time by 45–55 % and Swing paint by 20–33 % in every scene; fixed Metal lands within noise of Skia with Marlin tiles (12.1 vs 11.4, 13.2 vs 12.0, 10.5 vs 9.9, 9.5 vs 9.6 s). Of the original Metal→Skia-analytic paint gap, the glyph fix recovers 63 % (geometry: 4.9 → 1.8 s), 71 % (typing Java: 4.8 → 1.4 s), 79 % (Kotlin: 6.6 → 1.4 s), 59 % (rendering: 3.9 → 1.6 s).
2. **What is genuinely Skia's: the analytic shapes**, worth 1.4–1.8 s of paint per run (12–17 % of fixed Metal's paint) in shape-heavy scenes and ≈ 1.4 s in typing; visible in `AAShapePipe.renderTiles` 0.9 → 0.4 s plus the mask uploads they remove. Skia with Marlin tiles has no advantage over fixed Metal.
3. **Coalescing command buffers** is neutral once the stall is gone (geometry 12.1 vs 12.3, Java 13.2 vs 13.2) and slightly positive in Kotlin typing (10.5 → 8.8, within the wide spread); without the glyph fix it is harmful (§11.16 (4)). Not worth pursuing on its own.
4. Metal's remaining flush (4–5 s vs Skia's 3.4–4.0) is the per-tile mask upload path and the encoder restarts; a native profile after the glyph fix would locate it (candidates in §16).
5. Typing latency p90 and scene wall-clock remain unchanged across all six arms; the scenes keep the EDT 11–19 % busy, so all of this is EDT headroom, not visible latency.

**Consequence for the decision rule (§6):** the path-c control arm is now measured in the IDE: three small Metal fixes (§16) deliver 60–80 % of what the Skia backend showed against the shipped pipeline, with the remaining 12–17 % of paint time attributable to Skia's analytic shape rendering — the same quantity the planned Metal rrect/oval fast path (WP6) targets. Skia's performance case on macOS therefore rests on that last slice plus whatever a Metal `drawRRect` fast path cannot match; the dependency/platform arguments (Windows, Dawn) are untouched by this.

### 11.18 Windows on Graphite/Dawn, the Marlin-tile batching fix, and the Clear-composite cells (2026-10-15/16)

Windows (Parallels VM, Windows 11 ARM64, WDDM display adapter, feature level ≤ 11_1, no D3D12, no Vulkan): `libawt_skia` links against the `windows-Release-arm64` prebuilt (static CRT, Dawn + Tint inside, `awt_skia.dll` 10 MB vs 1.3 MB on macOS; `allocator_shim.lib` must not be linked, `mincore.lib` + `winmm.lib` added). Graphite/Dawn-D3D11 initializes, renders off-screen and presents. On-screen output is pixel-identical to the off-screen render; SwingSet2 and IDEA 2026.2.3 (whose bundled JBR is the exact base of the jbr25 branch) run correctly.

**Correctness (matrix at 1×):** `shapes=marlin`: 973 pass / 168 coverage-level / 11 suspect against the Mac's Skia-on-Metal sheet, 828 cells within 32 levels of software (Mac 853 then). Nine of the eleven were `comp:CLEAR/<gradient>/FILL_OVAL/AA`, the other two `comp:XOR/STROKE_W5[/AA]` (26 px, max 176, still open). In the analytic default: 969 / 181 / 2 against the Mac's analytic sheet, 800 within 32 of software (Mac 814).

**The Clear-composite cells, resolved on both sides.** Upstream `BufferedMaskFill.MaskFill` replaces every composite whose rule is not `SRC_OVER` with `AlphaComposite.SrcOver` before `validateContext`; the ops are `SET_ALPHA_COMPOSITE(SRC_OVER, USE_MASK)` + mask tiles, so every GPU pipeline that takes the Marlin-tile path (real Metal, Skia on Dawn, and now Skia on Metal) *draws the gradient*, while the software loops honour `Clear` and leave the oval black. On the Mac the old Skia-on-Metal sheet (2026-10-15 09:52) showed black; the current image (with the batching fix) draws the gradient: `ClearProbe` centre pixel `ff800080` on Skia/marlin and on Metal, `ff000000` on Skia/analytic and software; loop trace identical on both GPU pipelines (`SKMaskFill(OpaqueGradientPaint, SrcNoEa)` ×4 vs `MTLMaskFill(...)` ×4). The matrix is deterministic (three runs byte-identical). The black result was a property of the previous mask-tile path, not a Mac-only code path; it was never a Dawn defect (the standalone Dawn probe holds `kClear` in every variant). `analytic` and `skia` modes render the oval through Skia's own paths with the real `Clear` and match software. Java2D's SrcOver substitution for `Clear` + non-colour paints is a reference-pipeline quirk (→ §18): software clears, every GPU pipeline paints.

**Marlin tiles of many colours (shared code, `f73b005ec8db`).** On the VM a frame of 1000 differently coloured AA ovals made ~1,590 staging-row uploads of 64 KB and ~1,500 mesh draws for ~6,000 tiles: each colour started a new batch, each batch flush re-uploaded the whole 2048×32 staging row, and each fully covered interior tile left the batch for a `drawIRect`. Metal's unified memory hid the pattern (§11.11 only caught the per-tile variant); on D3D11 (upload ≈ 0.7 GB/s, ~10 µs per upload or draw) `FlatOvalAA` ran at 6 FPS vs GDI's 24. Fix: (1) upload only the dirty span of the staging row; (2) colour per vertex for atlas batches, from the second colour on (plain quads keep a batch per colour: a coloured-vertex mesh was slower than the separate draws they replace on this GPU, `WiredQuad` 40 vs 59; LCD/XOR blenders take the colour as a uniform and keep per-colour batches too); (3) fully covered tiles are sprites of a reserved opaque atlas cell. Per flush 1,590 uploads / 1,500 meshes → 95 / 3.5. VM RenderPerfTest: `FlatOvalAA` 6.3 → 24.0 FPS, `FlatQuadAA` 4.1 → 13.3, `RotatedOvalAA` 13.3 → 27.7, `WiredBubblesAA` 10.0 → 19.9 (GDI 23.8 / 10.8 / 24.6 / 19.5), geomean vs GDI 0.99× → 1.03×. **Mac matrix after the fix (2026-10-16): marlin 864 pass / 271 coverage / 17 suspect vs Metal, 844 within 32 of software (853 before — the nine Clear cells moved from software's side to Metal's); analytic 814 and skia 794 byte-identical to the 2026-10-15 sheets; the default mode now produces the analytic sheet.** Metal-side RenderPerfTest effect: see §12 (run of 2026-10-16).

**Windows RenderPerfTest shape (VM, 120 Hz, GDI | marlin | analytic | skia):** images and fills 2–5× over GDI; AA tiles ≈ GDI after the fix; analytic shapes at display rate (`FlatOvalAA` 24 | 24 | 118 | 120, `WiredBubblesAA` 19.5 | 19.6 | 120 | 117, gradient rotated ovals 17 | 7 | 120 | 58–77); general paths (`FlatQuadAA`, `WiredQuadAA`) at Marlin parity in analytic and 0.4× in `skia` without MSAA. Losses that are structural and shared with Metal: software-source blits (one queue flush per image, 0.1–0.2× GDI, which blits them on the CPU), XOR images, LCD text (per-colour batches by construction + ~6 ms CPU mesh build for 63k glyphs). Every losing number was a vblank multiple — the frame blocks in present several times per frame — but the present itself is cheap (acquire 0.01 ms, copy+submit ≤ 1 ms, present ≤ 0.6 ms; Mailbox ≈ FIFO).

### 11.19 Windows round 2: review items R10–R15 and the four leftover VM items (2026-10-16)

Review items (`DawnSKPlatform.{h,cpp}` rewritten around a shared `DawnSKDevice`): R10 error scopes (`Validation|OutOfMemory`) around `CreateTexture` and `Surface::Configure`, sizes checked against `maxTextureDimension2D` (16384 on the VM) — a 20000×64 VolatileImage falls back to `BufImgSurfaceData` with one WARNING; R11 `Win32SKGraphicsConfig.createSurfaceData` returns GDI unconditionally, a window leaving Skia has its surface flushed by the manager, and `FLUSH_SURFACE` disposes the platform side of WINDOW surfaces immediately (no-op on macOS); R12 `ImplicitDeviceSynchronization` requested when available, off-screen surfaces created under the queue lock; R13 shared atomic `lost` flag honoured by the `SKPlatform_*` entry points, `isDeviceLost()` → GDI surfaces and `replaceSurfaceDataLater()`; R14 `shared_ptr<DawnSKDevice>` in the surface; R15 `WindowsFlags` clears `d3dEnabled`/`d3dOnScreenEnabled` when Skia is requested. Shared: `SKGraphicsConfig` default methods (`colorModelFor`, `createSKVolatileImage`, caps) replace the WGL/MTL copies in `Win32SKGraphicsConfig`; `SKSurfaceData_PlatformOf(SurfaceDataOps*)`. Verified on the VM: window test pixel-identical through resize + repaint, SwingSet2, matrix 969/181/2 in the analytic default, huge-surface fallback, `d3d=true`+`skia=true`, IDEA with 0 ERROR lines. Not exercised: device loss (no safe TDR in the VM), cross-device sources (one adapter).

Leftover items: **`comp:XOR/STROKE_W5` resolved** — two wide XOR strokes crossing: software and Skia-on-Metal XOR the crossing twice, Dawn once, because both parallelograms of one paint shared a mesh and a runtime blender reads the destination (Metal: framebuffer fetch per fragment in order; Dawn/D3D11: destination copy once per draw). Fix in `SKQuadBatch`: one draw per primitive under a runtime blender (XOR, LCD); matrix on Dawn 973/179/**0** vs the Mac's analytic image; no Mac pixel changes. **`WiredQuad`** 40–46 FPS with σ ≈ 12 across runs (frames alternating between 2 and 3 vblanks at 120 Hz), not a regression — the Robot median flips between 60 and 40. **Pipeline-compile startup cost** (`StartupCost.java`): first off-screen frame 751 ms vs GDI 274, config creation 130 vs 89, first window present 1.21 s vs 0.58 s after JVM start → ≈ 0.6 s on the VM, not amortised across processes (no persistent pipeline cache on Dawn/D3D11). **`TextLCD`** is a §14 design item: LCD batches are per colour by construction (the blender takes the colour as a uniform; `src.rgb` is per-channel coverage) and 63k glyph sprites cost ≈ 6 ms CPU per frame; IDE frames have far fewer colours.

### 11.20 Windows rounds 3 and 4: overlap split, shared Dawn device, two-pass LCD (2026-10-19)

**Round 3 (VM).** The round-3 review items compiled and behaved on Dawn (`Device::GetLimits` returns `wgpu::Status` in the pinned Dawn); LCD probe 1789 px / max 2 against the software loops, matrix 974 / 178 / 0, SwingSet2 clean. **The overlap-only split does not rescue LCD text on Dawn**: every destination-reading draw costs ≈ 80–90 µs there regardless of size (`DstReadStrategy::kTextureCopy`: end the pass, copy, new pass), and adjacent LCD glyphs overlap by construction (the glyph images carry the filter's 1-px padding on both sides), so 100 lines of 84 glyphs became ≈ 8,500 passes: `LcdTextBench` 100 / 500 lines, ms per frame: software 1.4 / 5.8 (corrected numbers, 1×1 readback as the sync), no split (round 2, wrong at overlaps) 7.8 / 11.1, per-quad split 1214 / 5836, overlap-only split 770 / 4039, grayscale AA 7.1 / 9.4. Skia exposes no dual-source blending to runtime effects (Graphite's `DawnCaps` has the feature flag, used only by Skia's own LCD text; no public API, no open request found).

**Shared Dawn device (`06e94c106071`).** `DawnSK_CreateConfig` keeps a `std::shared_ptr<DawnSKDevice>` per backend under a mutex and reuses it unless lost; `DisplayChangeProbe` (two `Win32GraphicsDevice.displayChanged()` calls) yields three configurations on one device and a blit from an image of the first configuration into one of the third draws. With the environment-level `displayChanged()` the old images are invalidated by the change itself, as the volatile managers intend.

**Two-pass LCD (`098ad934afa5`, user decision to try it, then to make it the Windows default).** `sun.java2d.skia.lcd=twopass`: `SKQuadBatch` draws each LCD batch twice through `SKQuadBatchTwoPass` (kModulate with `1 − cov'`, kPlus with `colour · cov'`; runtime *shaders* over the atlas image shader, no destination read); the uncached LCD glyph path draws two rects; the batch is keyed by the paint's colour. `cov'` is the linear weight that reproduces the gamma blend (`contrast/100`) on white and on black, mixed by the channel's text value. Speed on the VM: 100 / 500 lines 2.7 / 8.9 ms (software 1.4 / 5.8, grayscale Skia 1.7 / 4.5; marginal per 1000 lines: software 11.4, two-pass 13.5, grayscale 6.9 ms). Fidelity (`LcdBackgrounds`, VM): exact (≤ 4) for black text everywhere and white on white/black/pale; white or gray-187 text on gray-128 / darcula-43 / navy max 13–15, mean 6–10; `blend` ≤ 4 everywhere.

**Mac verification and the overlap defect (2026-10-19).** Both lines build; matrices in all three modes byte-identical on both lines (the main-line marlin sheet keeps its 19 Clear/SrcIn `FILL_OVAL/AA` cells against jbr25, §11.18); window scene byte-identical at the display scale. `LcdBackgrounds` on Metal with `-Dsun.java2d.skia.lcd=twopass` (6 backgrounds × 5 colours, vs the software loops) reproduces the VM's numbers for single strings (max ≤ 35, mean ≤ 7). **With a second string overlapping the first (two glyph lists of one colour in one batch) the two-pass result was wrong: 600–700 px per cell > 32 levels, max 70–121, mean 27–35** (gray-187 on white: the overlap turns white; white on black: edges 76). Cause: both passes run over the whole mesh, so overlapping quads produce `dst·(1−c₁)(1−c₂) + s·c₁ + s·c₂` instead of the sequential `… + s·c₁(1−c₂) + s·c₂`, i.e. `s·c₁·c₂` too bright. Fix: two-pass batches split on overlap like blender batches (`splitOnOverlap = !blendMode || passes`); after it the overlap column matches the plain column (max ≤ 23, nothing > 32) and single strings improved too (white on gray-64: 35 → 24), since glyph boxes within one string overlap as well. **Consequence to measure on Dawn:** the split costs one draw pair per overlapping group but no destination copy; with the padding overlap of adjacent glyphs that is ≈ one pair per glyph, so `LcdTextBench` must be re-run (hand-over round 5). If too slow, the next step is a split on *ink* overlap (per-cell ink bounds above a small coverage threshold, computed at upload), which bounds the residual error to the filter tails.

### 11.4 WP 2.1 — code review of the prototype (2026-10-13)

An independent review (agent, full read of both commits against the writer-side Java/C, `MTLRenderQueue.m`/`MTLRenderer.m`/`shaders.metal`/`MTLLayer.m`, and the m156 headers) produced 17 findings; all were verified against the code and fixed in `fde94924097a`. The pattern is instructive for anyone writing a second backend against the `BufferedOpCodes` stream:

- **Encodings carry conventions that are only visible in the writer or the reference shader.** `DRAW_PARALLELOGRAM` passes the stroke *centerline*; `SET_GRADIENT_PAINT` maps the user's points to t = 0.25/0.75, not 0/1; gradient stop pixels are *premultiplied* (`PixelConverter.ArgbPre`) and may already be linear-RGB; `BEGIN_SHAPE_CLIP` *replaces* the rect clip without a `RESET_CLIP`. Each of these was a visible rendering difference in the first version. Lesson for 3.1: every opcode needs a golden case, and "renders plausibly" is not a test.
- **Graphite tracks dependencies through its own objects only.** Wrapping the same `MTLTexture` twice (`WrapBackendTexture` for the surface, `WrapTexture` for the image) gives Graphite two unrelated proxies, so a back-buffer→window blit in the same recording could be ordered before the back buffer's draws. `SkSurfaces::AsImage` carries the dependency. Metal avoids this class of problem by committing on every destination change.
- **Metal gives no ordering across command queues.** `MTLLayer.blitTexture` runs on `MTLContext.blitCommandQueue`; the Skia context now submits on that queue so presentation is ordered after rendering without a CPU wait. (Metal's own pipeline waits on the CPU for window destinations.)
- **Per-context state.** The Java `BufferedContext` caches what it last sent *per context*; a single native render state therefore needs a Java-side invalidation when the current context changes (`SkiaContext.validateContext` hides the static). Metal keeps native state per `MTLContext` instead; with a second display this matters.
- **Lifetime edges**: `DISPOSE_SURFACE`/`DISPOSE_CONFIG` in the same flush as draws; `flushAndInvokeNow` entry points run *after* `flushBuffer()` (own pool + end-of-flush); `LWCToolkit.sync()` and the GC disposer must drive the Skia queue too.
- **Build**: `SkTypes.h` selects `SK_DEBUG` unless `NDEBUG`/`SK_RELEASE` is defined — a fastdebug JDK against the Release prebuilt would silently mismatch class layouts. `-DSK_RELEASE` is now unconditional.
- Checked and found correct by the review: all wire formats (incl. `MASK_FILL` padding, `BLIT` packed params, text positions), struct mirrors in `SkiaMetalInterop.h`, radial-gradient focal mapping, texture-paint local matrix, alpha handling (`SET_COLOR` unpremul, extra alpha only for shaders/blits), clip-before-matrix ordering, Graphite API usage (non-deprecated overloads, readback sequence), ObjC/JNI lifetimes, lock ordering (MTL → Skia only), `validatePipe` otherwise mirrors MTL, `fetch_skia.sh` checksum/marker/trap logic.
- Also corrected: the `TODO` to move to `SkContexts::MakeGraphite` was wrong — in m156 `SkContext` exposes only `priv()`; `ContextFactory::MakeMetal` is the working entry point and is not deprecated (only `MtlGraphiteUtils.h` is). Noted in code instead.

Full jtreg subset on the final build (run 8, `build/jtreg-skia-8.log`): 253 passed / 10 failed — the four Metal-also (`GposTest`, `CopyAreaOOB`, `MenuMultiresolutionIconTest`, `PolyVertTest#opengl`), `GdiBlitOffscreenTest` (flaky, Metal-also), `SimplePrimQuality#opengl` (window-server blur), three `#id1` variants whose failing invocation carries `-Dsun.java2d.opengl=True` (see the OpenGL-flag finding below), and `SourceClippingBlitTest` (Robot-captured; failed in runs 1 and 8, passed in run 7 — re-run on both pipelines pending). **OpenGL-flag finding (user spotted the "OpenGL" window titles):** 16 tests in the subset pass `-Dsun.java2d.opengl=True`; on macOS `MacOSFlags` then selects the OpenGL pipeline when Metal is not explicitly enabled, and the Skia hook lives in the Metal configuration — so in every run so far (and in the Metal baselines) those invocations ran on OpenGL, with the Skia flag ignored. Fixed in `8fe4f99bd2ea` (jbr25 `4e0c51585ea6`): `sun.java2d.skia=true` implies Metal. Those 16 tests ran on Skia for the first time (`build/jtreg-skia-ogl.log`): **15 pass**, `PolyVertTest#opengl` fails as it does on Metal. `SourceClippingBlitTest` passed on Skia in that run and **fails on Metal** (`build/jtreg-metal-srcclip.log`) — a flaky Robot-capture test, moved to the Metal-also list. **Net after the review follow-up: zero Skia-specific jtreg failures in the subset.** IDEA 2026.2.3 started on the rebuilt jbr25 image (JRE 25.0.4+1-b508.27, 0 log errors, `startup-check-2.png`). jbr25 tip `4e0c51585ea6`; Skia sources and `MacOSFlags` identical to `main` tip `8fe4f99bd2ea`. - **2026-10-14** — **Review follow-up, part 1** (user: do all findings incl. optimizations). Commits on `main` so far: `b864d1becb49` seam fixes (H1–H5, M2), `978bd2a8ea3a` rendering correctness (M1, M3, M5, L1, L6, L8 + a new finding), `dced1a0e45d9` cleanup (L2–L5, L7, M4), `dd86fb1c0caa` one quad batch (opt. 4–6), `38c2b7ed344e` zero-copy uploads (opt. 2). Findings along the way: (a) **the matrix must run at `uiScale=1`** — on this Retina display `createCompatibleVolatileImage` renders at 2× and `getSnapshot` downsamples, so every stroke-level comparison against the software reference was confounded (lines looked shifted by one pixel on both GPU pipelines; a probe artefact); (b) at 1× the matrix found 40 REPEAT-gradient suspects where Metal matched software: **Skia samples paint shaders at pixel centres, Java's paint contexts and the Metal shaders (`position - 0.5`) at integer coordinates** — fixed with a half-pixel local matrix on every paint shader; (c) Bresenham now equals the software loops in every octant (Metal differs on 30 px per matrix cell), polylines shorten inner segments like `DrawPolygons.c`; (d) LCD text was confirmed never to have run on Skia (H1) and now differs from Metal by ≤ 1 level. Matrix at 1× after the fixes: Skia within 32 levels of software in 835 cells vs Metal 695; identical to software 248 vs 168 (classifier gained a "Skia at least as close as Metal" rule). Graphite copies upload pixels at record time (probe `upload_probe.mm`), which makes zero-copy uploads safe. Remaining from the review: opt. 3 (persistent upload surface — only if measurements still show it), opt. 7 (shape clip as region — Graphite converts regions to paths, no gain expected), opt. 8 (`drawEdgeAAQuad` experiment), `OPTIMIZATION := HIGH` measurement; then full jtreg + RenderPerfTest + jbr25 re-derivation + IDE check.

- **2026-10-16 (midday) — Metal PR #645 turned into the real fix: per-cell eviction.** Reviewer analysis (git history): Nikita Gubarkov owns the glyph-cache code (JBR-6208, JBR-6723 — which added the flush + commit + *wait* before freeing the cache —, JBR-7020); Alexey Ushakov the Metal pipeline; Laurent Bourgès the texture pool. Branch rebased `--onto origin/main` (PR showed 1,880 commits: our local `main` was an older rewritten generation of JBR's `main`); commit cites JBR-6723 and the retained-references guarantee of `[MTLCommandQueue commandBuffer]`; `MTLTR_SyncFlushGlyphVertexCache` → `MTLTR_CommitGlyphVertexCache`. **Per-cell eviction (user: do the real fix, not the cheap one), implemented in `build/wt-metal`:** command buffers are numbered in commit order (`MTLCommandBufferWrapper.sequence`, `MTLContext.commandBufferSequence` = buffer being recorded, `completedCommandBufferSequence` = atomic max from the three completion handlers — `MTLContext`, `MTLLayer`, `MTLBlitLoops`); each cell records `lastUsed` = sequence of the buffer that last drew it; `addGlyph` reuses a cell when the texture is full with `AccelGlyphCache_AddGlyph`'s policy (head-to-tail walk, `timesRendered < 5` or empty, counters reset, two passes) plus the Metal-specific constraint **`lastUsed <= completed`** (replaceRegion is a CPU write; a cell still referenced by a recorded or running buffer is skipped); when no cell qualifies (a frame with more distinct glyphs than cells) the renderer falls back to the fresh-texture replacement, now without the GPU wait. 1024² atlas and plain layout kept. Build on the new base in progress; checks: 1152-cell matrix vs shipped Metal, `GlyphChurn` (3,000 distinct glyphs over 40 frames + one frame above the cell count, gray and LCD) byte-compared against the unmodified Metal image.

- **2026-10-16 (afternoon) — JBR-7462 and the eviction review.** User question: is JBR-7462 "MTLGlyphCache free called too often" (Laurent Bourgès, 2024, assigned avu, Submitted) related? Yes — same free-and-reallocate with the JBR-6723 wait, triggered by destination changes (`SET_SURFACES`/`SET_SCRATCH_SURFACE`/`DISPOSE_SURFACE` free both caches) rather than by a full cache. Laurent's live branch `lbourges/JBR-7462-main-V2` (23458449171c, 2026-09-14, 22 files incl. unrelated thread-handling work): `flush` vs `free` split, texture from `MTLTexturePool`, flush callback without commit/wait, flush instead of free at destination changes, `GlyphCacheSurfaceSwitchTest` (< 4 ms per switch). **User decision: combine both, keeping his authorship** → his glyph-cache part hand-ported (without instrumentation, with the pooled texture *registered* with the command buffer before release so the pool keeps it busy until completion — his prototype released it immediately) as a commit authored `bourgesl <bourges.laurent@gmail.com>` citing the source commit, then our eviction on top. **Second review of the eviction diff (`build/notes/review-metal-eviction-2026-10-16.md`):** B1 sticky `isFull` and B2 `cellCount` init (both already found and fixed via the glyph-churn test — the first build rendered overlapping glyphs), B3 guard the fallback when re-validation fails, R1 the flush callback must not commit (JBR-6723's waits after frees would find no wrapper) and the completion handlers must not retain the context (`dealloc` on a Metal thread) → counter moves into a small object shared by context and wrappers, R2 in-order completion premise to document, R3 early-out after a pass without idle cells. LCD path removal (Laurent's TODO on the ticket): separate ticket; `LWCToolkit` still publishes `LCD_HRGB` as the desktop hint, so applications asking for system AA use the Metal LCD path today.

- **2026-10-16 (afternoon) — combined Metal glyph-cache series verified.** Laurent's part (flush vs free, pooled texture registered with the command buffer, flush at destination changes) + our eviction + review fixes (completion counter in `MTLCompletedCommandBuffers` shared by context and wrappers — no handler retains the context; fallback guard; early exit; `reusableCell` private; in-order-completion premise documented). Checks on the JDK 28 image vs the unmodified Metal image: **glyph-churn scene byte-identical (gray and LCD, mid and last frame)**; matrix 1144 pass / 4 coverage / 4 suspect, all gradient `DRAW_OVAL` noise; `GlyphCacheSurfaceSwitchTest` 0.045 ms per switch vs 0.415 ms unmodified (both under the 4 ms bound). **Micro-benchmark `GlyphChurnBench` (median ms per frame, old → new):** 200 distinct glyphs (no eviction) gray 5.60 → 5.00, LCD 5.19 → 5.11; 600 glyphs (old: wholesale replacement every frame; new: fits the 1024-cell cache) gray 6.27 → 5.06, LCD 6.89 → 5.57; 1500 glyphs (new: per-cell eviction each frame) gray 7.75 → 5.53, LCD 9.69 → 6.62. - **2026-10-20 (afternoon) — grayscale text numbers for the daily, and the atlas-eviction regression they exposed.** `GlyphChurnBench` with a one-pixel readback as the sync (the full-frame snapshot it used before added 4 to 5 ms to every GPU arm and hid the per-glyph cost), median ms per frame at 1x, 200 / 600 / 1,500 / 3,000 distinct glyphs: software loops 0.16 / 0.25 / 0.79 / 2.26; Metal shipped 0.45 / 1.45 / 3.23 / 6.83; Metal with PR #645 0.42 / 0.44 / 1.08 / 2.27; **Skia 0.48 / 0.51 / 130 / 243**. Reading: the loops composite text about 3x faster per glyph than either GPU pipeline; Skia and fixed Metal are equal while the atlas holds the frame; above 1,024 cells Skia fell off a cliff. Cause: yesterday's glyph-list fix made every eviction correct by going through AccelGlyphCache's flush callback, which ended the recording per overwritten cell (2,800 recordings per frame), and every glyph went up in its own `writePixels` (an upload task recorded and a GPU blit replayed per glyph; `sample`: `UploadTask::addCommands` and `Context::submit` dominated once the flushes were gone). **Fix, test first** (`GlyphAtlasEvictionThroughputTest`: frame time at 1,500 glyphs over 600, limit 6x; red at 226x gray / 218x LCD, Metal 1.8x): (1) per-cell eviction dated by a recording generation (`SKConfigContext::recordingGeneration`, advanced in `SKBackend_FlushRecording`; a cell last used in an earlier recording is overwritten without a flush, since an upload in the next recording follows every draw of the previous; the recording is ended once only when every cell was used in the current one; counted: 1.7 walk steps per miss, 2 flushes per frame at 3,000 glyphs); (2) a CPU shadow of the atlas with per-row dirty rects uploaded in `SKQuadBatch::beforeUpload` when the batch draws (the mask cache's staging idea), instead of one `writePixels` per glyph. **Result: 1,500 glyphs 130 to 1.5 ms (ratio 2.5 gray, 3.2 LCD), 3,000 glyphs 243 to 4.7 ms; frames that fit unchanged; all correctness tests green.** Commit `glyph atlas evicts per cell without a flush and uploads rows, not glyphs` (`short/24.txt`) on both lines. For the daily: Skia's text advantage in the IDE scenes was Metal's glyph-cache stall (fixed in PR #645); what is Skia's own is the analytic shapes; per glyph the CPU loops win in isolation, the whole frame goes to the GPU because fills, images and shapes are 2 to 5x faster there and any mixing would cost a sync worth more than the text saving. Both lines rewritten (22 commits; the new commit before the Metal switches), jtreg 9/9 on both images, matrices and window scene unchanged; tips on the fork: main `0b1aae3cf5d3` (on `origin/main` `b003cc37a2ee`), jbr25 `e7badf8526c6` (on `58dd0d708031`).
- **2026-10-21 — PR #645 review (Nikita Gubarkov): the texture layout question, answered with a test and a flag.** Nikita asked whether per-cell `replaceRegion` into a texture other cells of which the GPU is sampling is safe, and noted the PR set `allowGPUOptimizedContents = NO` only for A8 pool textures. Findings: the BGRA (LCD) glyph cache was indeed left tiled, by the pool's format rule, although it is written the same way; the macOS desktop font hints default to `LCD_HRGB` (`LWCToolkit.initializeDesktopProperties`), so the BGRA cache is the default text path of plain Swing applications on JBR even though CoreText's glyphs are colour-neutral there. **Test first:** `GlyphCacheConcurrentUploadTest` commits a heavy frame (400 translucent full-screen AA ovals plus a glyph set), then uploads four other glyph sets into neighbouring cells of the same cache before it can complete, and requires the frame to equal the one drawn alone; timing shows the frame still running 12 to 15 ms after the uploads, so the overlap is real. Result on the PR as it was: **0 of 150 iterations differ in grayscale and in LCD**, 600 upload frames each, tiled BGRA included; evidence, not proof, that the Apple driver's tiled upload does not touch neighbouring blocks. **Fix anyway** (the Metal documentation promises nothing at sub-resource granularity): an explicit `linear:` request on `MTLTexturePool getTexture`, part of the pooled item's key so a linear texture is never handed to a render-target request; both glyph caches and the mask cache ask for it, the A8 format rule is gone, the compiled-out shared-pool path gets an adapter. All six glyph-cache tests pass on the rebuilt image; churn benchmark unchanged (gray 0.46 / 1.11 / 2.29 ms at 600 / 1,500 / 3,000). **Pre-existing, not ours:** LCD churn above ~2,000 distinct glyphs costs 24 to 67 ms per frame on the shipped pipeline and 18 to 61 on the PR, where grayscale stays at 2 ms; glyphs taller than the 32-px cells take the uncached per-glyph texture path (§13). PR branch folded and **force-pushed to `origin`** (`6e4e231a4c6b` Laurent + `25a94fc888ca` + `cf8313c18c58`, messages updated); the GitHub PR description was left as is, the user reviews in Space now.

Draft replies to both review comments are in the session notes: the stall was a GPU wait on every surface-pair change (`SET_SURFACES` fires on any source or destination change, so per icon blit), a cell is overwritten only after every command buffer that sampled it has completed, the old `free` was synchronous (flush callback committed and waited), not racy. **CI builds started (2026-10-21, evening):** `JBR_Dev_Main_MacosAarch64nomod` build 1101559003 and `JBR_Dev_Main_WindowsX64nomod` build 1101559005, branch `manuel.unterhofer/JBR-10654-skia-backend`, `WITH_SKIA=1` (https://buildserver.labs.intellij.net/build/1101559003, https://buildserver.labs.intellij.net/build/1101559005); the Windows one is the first `--with-skia` configure of `mkimages_x64.sh` ever. JBRperf `-skia*` options held back until the text-rendering knobs settle; proposal to Sergey later: one generic pass-through of `-D` options into `J2D_OPTS` rather than per-backend flags. **Render chain finding:** the Render jobs do not consume the `nomod` builds. Their artifact dependency takes `jbrsdk-28-<os>-<arch>-bN.tar.gz` and the `-test-` tarball from `JBR_Dev_Main_Bundle`, a composite with snapshot dependencies on all nine platform builds (`JBR_Dev_Main_MacosAarch64`, `…WindowsX64`, Linux glibc/musl, Windows aarch64, macOS x64) plus `Tests_CommitTesting_Bunch`, with `sync-revisions`, so a Render run on our branch queues that whole chain on our branch. The nomod builds therefore only prove the `WITH_SKIA=1` build path (they do produce the same artifact names). To run Render on Skia: `jb tc native run start JBR_Dev_Main_Tests_Performance_Render_macOS15aarch64Metal --branch manuel.unterhofer/JBR-10654-skia-backend -P reverse.dep.JBR_Dev_Main_MacosAarch64.env.WITH_SKIA=1 -P reverse.dep.JBR_Dev_Main_WindowsX64.env.WITH_SKIA=1 -E 'JAVA_OPTS=-esa -Djbr.catch.SIGABRT=true -Dsun.java2d.metal=true -Dsun.java2d.skia=true'` (the job's `env.JAVA_OPTS` is `-esa -Djbr.catch.SIGABRT=true -Dsun.java2d.metal=true`; Skia on macOS rides on the Metal pipeline, so `metal=true` stays; `render.options` adds `-metal` through `run_inc.sh`, which is the same property). Unverified: whether the full `JBR_Dev_Main_MacosAarch64` / `…WindowsX64` jobs build through `mkimages.sh` / `mkimages_x64.sh` (their steps are not visible through the REST API with my permissions; the build logs did not download through the CLI). The second Render run compares against the first Render build on the same branch (`ScoresComparator` against the previous build), so the baseline run is the same command without the Skia property, or the main-branch history. **Results (16:38):** macOS nomod build 1101559003 **green** (artifacts `jbrsdk-28-osx-aarch64-b4682.tar.gz` and the `-test-` tarball, the first Skia image built on JetBrains infrastructure). Windows nomod build 1101559005 **failed in `fetch_skia.sh`**: the agents are Cygwin (`CYGWIN_NT-10.0-17763`) and the `curl` in PATH is the native Windows one, which cannot open the `/cygdrive/z/...` temp path (`curl: (23) client returned ERROR on write`), so `SKIA_DIR` stayed empty and `mkimages_x64.sh` exited 23. Fix folded into `the perf and image scripts learn the Skia backend`: the download target goes through `cygpath -w` when available, and unpacking falls back to Python's `zipfile` when `unzip` is missing (configure already fixes the `--with-skia` path up with `UTIL_FIXUP_PATH`). No jtreg test for this one: it is a build-script defect on a specific agent environment; the TeamCity build is the test. Branch tip now `c3b13cf7565d` on `origin` and the fork (tag `pre-fixup-fetch-skia` = `52818e6b7060` locally). Restarted as build **1101596080** (https://buildserver.labs.intellij.net/build/1101596080). **Perf pipeline facts corrected (DSL read from `ssh://git@git.jetbrains.team/ij/runtime-teamcity-config.git`, branch `jbr-dev`, `.teamcity/src/jbr/tests/performance/`):** the `mini_Metal`/`mini_OGL` Render jobs (agent munit-760) are `enabled = false` since March; the live macOS 15 aarch64 jobs run on `intellij-macos-hw-munit-924` (template `JBR_TCAgentMacOSStudioAarch64`), the same agent for Render, J2DBench, Mapbench, SwingMark Metal and OGL. The `JBR_Dev_Main_<os>` jobs are composites of `<os>debug` + `<os>nomod` (`OsComposite.kt`), so the nomod build is the artifact source of the chain and `reverse.dep.JBR_Dev_Main_MacosAarch64nomod.env.WITH_SKIA=1` is the right knob. Render runs `run_rp.sh <mode> %render.options% -n 3 <title>` with RenderPerfTest `-t` (milliseconds per frame, not FPS) for 17 titles × 3 modes; `render.options`, `render.modes`, `env.JAVA_OPTS` are plain parameters a custom run can override. **Two findings about the pipeline itself:** (1) every Render number on macOS and Windows in the history is 16.6–16.9 ms, i.e. vsync-locked (display sync on), so the pipeline as configured does not discriminate between backends; `-dSync false` in `render.options` turns it off (`-Dsun.java2d.metal.displaySync=false`, which the Skia backend inherits because it presents through `MTLLayer`). (2) Since 2026-10-20 both the Metal and the OGL Render jobs on munit-924 hang in the first on-screen test (`ClipFlatOval_onscreen`, 240 min execution timeout, JVM prints its properties and nothing more) while J2DBench on the same agent passes; the macOS 14 aarch64 Render jobs fail on a missing `python3`. The on-screen mode is the Robot-timed one, so this is an agent problem (screen capture permission or window focus), not a pipeline one; to report to the perf infrastructure owners. **First Skia Render run queued: build 1101613691** (https://buildserver.labs.intellij.net/build/1101613691), branch ours, main's b4680 builds reused for every dependency except the macOS aarch64 composite and its nomod build (rebuilt with `WITH_SKIA=1`), `render.modes=-volatile -buffer` (no on-screen test), `render.options=-metal -dSync false`, `JAVA_OPTS` with `-Dsun.java2d.metal=true -Dsun.java2d.skia=true`. The Metal baseline is the same command on the same Bundle without the Skia property; the comparison is read from the two `render_perf.txt` artifacts, not from the job's own comparator (whose history is the vsync-locked main line). **Correction (same evening):** that chain resolved wrongly. With explicit `--reuse-deps`, TeamCity rebuilt the macOS composite but filled its nomod dependency with main's finished build 1100707691 (no Skia), so builds 1101613691 (Skia options) and 1101617249 (Metal) both run on the stock main image b4680; they cannot be cancelled with my permissions (`run cancel` and `DELETE /app/rest/buildQueue` both refused), so they will deliver two Metal-on-stock-main baselines with display sync off and the two offscreen modes, ~13 min each. Two chain rules learned: every build in a customised chain must share the same `BuildCounter` build (mixing #4682 and #4680 is rejected: "the same build configuration has different builds"), and a dependency that is not listed is filled with any suitable finished build, so the Skia nomod build must be listed explicitly. Hence a second Skia nomod build **1101619980** was started with `--reuse-deps 1100707652` (counter #4680) and `WITH_SKIA=1`, and the Skia Render run reuses it by id together with main's #4680 builds for every other platform. **That is rejected too:** `Tests_CommitTesting_Bunch` (reused, 288 min) pins main's nomod build through its ~40 jtreg jobs, so any chain that reuses the Bunch carries main's macOS image, and any chain that does not reuses nothing of value (the Bunch depends on every platform image). Conclusion: **with the current DSL, a Render run on a Skia image means the full chain on our branch** (BuildCounter, six OS composites = twelve debug+nomod builds, the Bunch, Bundle; ~6–7 h wall clock, ~15 agent-hours), or a DSL change in `runtime-teamcity-config` (maintainer of the perf DSL since 2025: Sergey Shelomentsev) that gives a Render variant an artifact dependency on the `<os>nomod` build instead of the Bundle. The spare Skia nomod build 1101619980 (counter #4680) is harmless and gives a second Skia image. Windows build 1101596080 passed the download step with the Cygwin fix and is in configure. **User decision (2026-10-21, evening): run the full chain.** Render build **1101650902** on our branch with no reused dependencies: Bundle 1101650901 → BuildCounter 1101650699, composites LinuxX64 1101650707, MacosX64 1101650715, WindowsX64 1101650721 (nomod 1101650716, `WITH_SKIA=1`), MacosAarch64 1101650712 (nomod 1101650711, `WITH_SKIA=1`), LinuxAarch64 1101650703, WindowsAarch64 1101650719, Musl 1101650709/1101650708, CommitTesting_Bunch 1101650900 (runs the jtreg suites, incl. our ten tests, on the Skia-enabled images with Skia off). Render parameters: `render.options=-metal -dSync false`, `render.modes=-volatile -buffer`, `JAVA_OPTS` with `-Dsun.java2d.skia=true`. The Metal baseline is queued on the same Bundle (reuse of a queued Bundle is accepted). Windows build 1101596080 configured with `--with-skia` (SkCanvas.h found, make running) — the Cygwin fix is confirmed. **Agent displays:** the macOS 15 agent (Mac Studio class: 20 cores, 128 GB, macOS 15.8.1) screenshots a 1920×1080 desktop, the Windows 11 hardware agent two 1920×1080 desktops; the 16.7 ms plateau is a real 60 Hz display pipeline (monitor or dummy plug, indistinguishable from here), not a simulated clock. Topic for Sergey: all Render history on macOS/Windows is vsync-locked; the jobs need `-dSync false` (Metal) and the equivalent for other pipelines, or the `-f` FPS/robot mode must be dropped. **Correction and the measurement matrix (same evening):** `env.JAVA_OPTS` is not read by any macOS perf script (only the Windows Render script and MapBench, which overwrites it with `J2D_OPTS`), so Render runs 1101650902, 1101651124, 1101613691 and 1101617249 are all Metal (the first two on the Skia-enabled image b4685, the last two on stock b4680). The backend is switched with `-E JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=...`, which the JVM reads directly; all score parsers (`tail -n1`, `awk /averaged/`, `awk /Score/`, `grep -A5 Scores`, title grep) tolerate the "Picked up JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS" line. Chain build 1101650711 (macOS nomod) finished green with `--with-skia` in its configure line, artifacts `*-b4685.tar.gz`. Why the other jobs and not only Render: Metal vs OpenGL on the same agent differ in every one of them (J2DBench poly250 2.7e9 vs 3.3e9 units/s; JavaDraw 1376 vs 2468 FPS on bitmaps, 81 vs 90 on 4000 circles; SwingMark 28,002 vs 147,075 ms; MapBench 49 vs 31 FPS on circles), so none is refresh-capped, unlike Render; DaCapo and SPECjbb2015 are JVM benchmarks and irrelevant. **Queued on Bundle 1101650901 (macOS 15 aarch64, agent munit-924), user-approved:** Render skia 1101703828, skia+shapes 1101703932; J2DBench metal 1101704036, skia 1101704140, shapes 1101704244; JavaDraw metal 1101704356, skia 1101704482, shapes 1101704806; SwingMark metal 1101705015, skia 1101705205, shapes 1101705320; MapBench metal 1101705424, skia 1101705528, shapes 1101705632 (`shapes` = `-Dsun.java2d.skia.shapes=skia`). Results are read pairwise from the `*_perf.txt` artifacts and the per-iteration logs in `*_log.zip`, not from the jobs' history comparators (Metal history). The queue behind the hanging OpenGL Render run (240 min timeout) delays everything on that agent.

(2) `WindowsFlags` accepted `t`/`True`/empty for `sun.java2d.skia` where `SKEnvironment` accepts only `true`: `-Dsun.java2d.skia=t` disabled Direct3D without enabling Skia → `SKEnvironment.isSkiaRequested()` used by both platform flag classes. (3) Native: a failed glyph/mask cache creation was retried and logged on every glyph list / tile (now once per context), a failed LCD table upload was cached as null forever, `makeRecorder`/`AsImage` results unchecked. **Behaviour alignments with Metal:** texture-to-screen blits no longer flush the queue (only rendered-surface sources do, `MTLBlitLoops`' rule); the queue flusher only flushes on timeout when the buffer holds work; `isSkiaEnabled()` is lock-free after init. **Protocol:** the stroke record shrank from `6 + dashes` to 4 floats (dashes never reached the native side). **Dead code removed:** `gpuApiNative()` and the `gpuApi` parameter (both platform implementations), `path(…, normalize)`, `getSKGraphicsConfig()`, unused imports/includes, the `TwoPass` alias, duplicated flush/clear/span/source checks folded into helpers (`flushBatchesOf`, `flushAtlasBatches`, `sourceUsable`, `lcdCoverageShader`, `drawSpans`). **Docs:** ~15 stale comments corrected (shared Dawn device, SKPlatform's Windows half, flush-on-eviction, upload vs stage, `SKShapePipe` class doc, `defaultsFor`), `lib-skia.m4` checks the header before the path fixup, `mkimages_aarch64.sh` learned `WITH_SKIA`, `-o` only when `SKIA_PREBUILT_ROOT` is set. **Tests:** shared `PipelineComparison` helper (guard, reference, rendered snapshot, compare with missing-ink), short JetBrains headers everywhere; `LcdTextLayersTest` summary rewritten (it cannot reach the 16-bit layer split; a Metal run line measured **700 px up to 135 levels off** — Metal's LCD batch reads a destination copy taken before the batch, so overlapping glyphs in one batch are wrong: recorded in §16 as a Metal candidate, left out with a sentence), `LcdTextDisposeTest` dispose half made deterministic via `SurfaceData.invalidate()`, `AnalyticShapeEdgeTest` image enlarged so the big ellipse's far edges are inside at 2×, `NonAAGeometryTest` per-cell edge budget on the run line (measured 127 on the rotated stroked rect, 50 sheared, < 30 elsewhere, identical on Skia and Metal → 160), throughput test alternates fitting/evicting frames with `/timeout=120`. **Verification:** the Skia and Metal render matrices are byte-identical to the pre-cleanup image (1152 cells each); 15/15 jtreg tests pass on the cleanup image (the 10 tests + `PipelineSelectionTest` + 4 pre-existing). Lesson in memory: fixups carry final-tree context and conflict at early targets; resolve by re-applying the intended edit, never `--ours`. Tag `pre-cleanup-rewrite` = `c3b13cf7565d`. Final rebuild on the rewritten tip green, **15/15 jtreg tests pass**; pushed to `origin` and the fork as **`744a6d17bba5`** (25 commits on `origin/main` `beee138cfe19`). Next round's `REWRITE_BASE`: `beee138cfe19`.

The chain itself finished: Bundle 1101650901 green, both Windows Skia builds green (standalone 1101596080 and in-chain 1101650716, the Cygwin fix confirmed), the Bunch's 494 failures are agent state (`unit-2181` ran the macOS AWT suite: 382 focus/key-event failures vs 2 on main's agent; the same agent produced main's 199 Swing failures the day before; Windows 11 aarch64 AWT/Swing +117 on `jre-w11a-azr7/8`). User decisions: strokes both ways behind a property; font engine = the prebuilt's platform hosts; follow-ups parked in §13. **`sun.java2d.skia.strokes=skia|java`** (commit `a72176186cc6`): `java` routes every stroke through Java's stroker and fills the outline (the dashed route); it had to bypass the analytic branch too, which otherwise strokes ovals and round rects itself. Visual (`build/skia-smoke/StrokeCompare.java`, sheet `strokes-compare-2026-10-22.png`, 1 px and 3 px strokes at 1x/2x vs the software loops, max levels / px beyond 32): rotated round rect 1 px 2x **88 / 270 → 46 / 4**, oval 1 px 2x 68 / 16 → 31 / 0, pill 1 px 2x 40 / 14 → 34 / 1, oval 3 px 44 / 13 → 37 / 3; slightly worse on axis-aligned round rects at 2x (16 → 28, 15 → 23), identical on paths and at 1 px 1x (≤ 1). Metal (Marlin tiles) is ≤ 1 level everywhere. `AnalyticShapeEdgeTest` passes with `strokes=java` at 8 / 32 (new run line). **Cost (RenderPerfTest `-t -e=volatile`, display sync off, M1 Max):** `WiredBubblesAA` (1,000 stroked AA ovals per frame) 2.4 ms with Skia strokes vs **20.4 ms** with Java strokes (8.5×, ≈ 18 µs per stroked oval: the stroker on the EDT plus a path through Graphite's path atlas instead of an analytic draw); `WiredBoxAA`, `WiredQuadAA` (parallelogram pipe) and the non-AA `WiredBubbles` unchanged. Local lesson: RenderPerfTest's mode flag is `-e=volatile`; `-evolatile` silently selects the Robot mode, which hangs here exactly as on the CI agent. Context for the same scene: Marlin tiles through Skia 31.8 ms, Metal (Marlin tiles) 32.4 ms, software loops into a BufferedImage 16.1 ms — so Java strokes filled by Skia (20.4) still beat both GPU Marlin routes and sit 1.3× behind the pure software loops; Skia's analytic strokes (2.4) are the only route that is faster than the CPU.

Not in the FPS numbers but a user-visible one-second freeze each. - ~~`REPEAT` gradients: seam pixels~~ — root-caused and fixed (`67696f8f9c23`): Java's float t lands on the integer at a cycle boundary and wraps; Skia's evaluation order leaves it an ulp below and shows the last stop; a 1e-5 bias in t makes the boundary pixel wrap the same way (radial seams now identical to software and Metal). Linear REPEAT seams differ from software by one pixel on *both* GPU pipelines because the software result itself is float-rounding noise (seam at x=70 but exact at x=130 in the probe) — not fixable, not ours. Matrix (marlin mode, 1×): 853 cells within 32 levels of software, 2 flagged (`DRAW_RECT/AA`: AA parallelogram ring via path atlas vs Metal's analytic shader, 8 px ≤ 44 levels — candidate: analytic `drawRect`/`drawDRRect` for axis-aligned AA parallelograms).
- ~~MSAA off (§10): re-run RenderPerfTest for AA-parallelogram-heavy tests~~ — done (§11.7b): 25 % on AA parallelograms only. Still open: variant 2 must be measured both ways (`sun.java2d.skia.msaa=4`); consider routing AA parallelograms to `drawRect`/`drawDRRect` under the transform (analytic renderer, no atlas, no MSAA) instead of `drawPath`.

Variant 1 is at or near Metal everywhere RenderPerfTest looks (§11.7, geomean 1.04×). Possible further step: `sk_FragCoord` via private access for gradient-painted tiles.

- Gradient dithering: Skia and Metal differ by ±1–4 levels across gradients (stripe pattern in the diff heat map); decide in 3.1 whether this is "imperceptible" by definition.
- Verify two-Skia isolation on Linux (IJ + Compose plugin + Skia backend) once `libawt_skia` exists; add `-Bsymbolic`/version script to the target.
- Graphite probe timing varies between runs (0.276 vs 0.049 ms/frame for the same workload) — first-run pipeline compilation vs. warm; benchmark harness must warm up and report distributions.

- **Windows (from the VM session):** `comp:XOR/STROKE_W5[/AA]` 26 px / max 176 Dawn vs Skia-on-Metal — the only remaining matrix suspect on Dawn. Lost-device protocol (D3D9's `setSurfaceLost`/restore/GDI fallback) not implemented; Dawn's device-lost callback only logs. Pipeline-compile startup cost ≈ 180 ms over the first frames (traces) — measure with first-present timing. `WiredQuad` 43 vs 59 FPS between runs on the VM, unexplained (not present mode, not the batching commit; GDI stable) — re-check on a quiet VM. `TextLCD` 0.1–0.2× GDI: per-colour batches by construction (LCD blender takes the colour as a uniform) + ~6 ms CPU mesh build per frame for 63k glyphs. Software blits / XOR images: one queue flush per image (structural, same on Metal). Real Windows hardware (NVIDIA/AMD/Intel Xe/Snapdragon X), RDP and Hyper-V still unmeasured.
- `lib-skia.m4`: Windows needs both Dawn include directories (`third_party/externals/dawn/include` and `out/<cfg>/gen/third_party/dawn/include`); `allocator_shim.lib` must stay unlinked. Document in the build notes when the configure flag is documented.

- **Second independent review (2026-10-16, `build/notes/review-2026-10-16.md`), deferred items:** (R2) cross-context sources are wrapped with the destination's recorder — valid on macOS (one Metal device), to verify on Windows where each config owns a `wgpu::Device` (drag a window with a cached TexturePaint across monitors; fix = read back + re-upload when `src->cc != g.cc`). (R7) presentation is not serialised with the Skia queue: `MTLLayer.drawInMTLContext` holds only the Metal lock while the backend submits mid-flush → a torn frame is possible in screenshots; fix natively by deferring `submit` to `EndOfFlush` where no CPU wait is needed. (R8) `pTexture`/`layer` read without the Metal lock in `MTLSKPlatform.mm` (benign, same race as Metal's flusher); forward `invalidate()` after the Skia `FLUSH_SURFACE`. (R9) `MTLSKInterop.h` hand-mirrors `MTLGraphicsConfigInfo`/`MTLSDOps` — move the plain structs into a Cocoa-free header shared by both libraries. Windows: (R10) Dawn returns error objects, not null — `PushErrorScope`/`PopErrorScope` around texture creation and `Configure` for a real GDI fallback; (R11) a `bbNum != 0` or GDI fallback leaves the old swap chain configured until GC; (R12) off-screen `CreateTexture` on the caller's thread without the queue lock (request `ImplicitDeviceSynchronization` or lock); (R13) device-lost only logs; (R14) `DawnSKSurface::config` raw pointer (hold device/queue by value); (R15) `-Dsun.java2d.d3d=true` + skia → silent GDI (clear `d3dEnabled` in `WindowsFlags`). Nits kept for later: `SKRenderQueue` is a near-verbatim `MTLRenderQueue` copy (B3 in §16), `MTLSKEnvironment.mm` throwaway device for the description, `NSAutoreleasePool` instead of hand-declared `objc_autoreleasePool*`, hard-coded colour type in `MTLSKPlatform.mm:85`, Windows config Javadoc copies that belong in `share/` as `SKGraphicsConfig` defaults, Dawn INFO traces per resize → VERBOSE. Fixed the same day: R1, R3, R4, R5, R6 (comment), dead dash branch, unused `dirty`, `SKContext.forConfig` per batch, stale XOR comment, duplicate `defaultsFor` branches, B1, B2, R16, unused spec vars, R17 (sticky flag), R18 (size validation), and all eight external references.
- **Decision to confirm: `libawt_skia` is built with `OPTIMIZATION := LOW`**, like `libawt_lwawt` (Metal) and unlike `libawt` (HIGHEST). Even against Metal; a CI run on HIGH would measure the interpreter's own cost more fairly against the software loops. Keep LOW for the Metal comparison, revisit for a Marlin-vs-Skia-raster comparison.

- **RenderPerfTest hang in analytic mode on `RotatedBox_XOR` (2026-10-16, `skia-hang3-jstack.txt`):** the test's frame-marker wait never returns while EDT and flusher are idle — the robot never sees the marker pixel; the skipped-frames item above is the mild form. Reproduce with `-Dsun.java2d.skia.shapes=analytic` on the XOR tests alone; check the XOR blender + analytic shape path for a frame that is never presented.

- **Windows (round 2 leftovers):** device-loss path (R13) untested — needs a TDR on real hardware; cross-device sources (R2) need two adapters; pipeline-compile startup ≈ 0.6 s on the VM — a persistent pipeline cache (Dawn `CacheDevice`/`DawnCacheDeviceDescriptor`) is the candidate; `TextLCD` per-colour batches → §14 design item.

- **Windows (review round 2, for the VM):** A3 device-loss recovery, A4 one draw per glyph under runtime blenders on dst-copy backends (verify first), A6 cross-device source blits, A8 GDI surface churn while lost. Metal switches: `COMP_ISCOPY` not coalesced (documented, decide when the switch is ever upstreamed).

- ~~**Windows follow-up (review round 3 R1)**~~ done 2026-10-19 (VM round 4, `06e94c106071`, §11.20): one `wgpu::Device` per adapter shared by all configurations; today each `displayChanged` creates a device and invalidates every VolatileImage. Hand-over round 4.
- **Metal LCD text with glyphs taller than the 32-px cells** (sizes above ~28 px at 1x): the uncached per-glyph texture path makes LCD frames with more than ~2,000 distinct glyphs cost 24 to 67 ms on the shipped pipeline (grayscale 2 ms); pre-existing, seen 2026-10-21 with `GlyphChurnBench … lcd`. Candidate for a later Metal PR (larger cells for big glyphs, or a pooled staging texture).
- **Analytic strokes vs Marlin's stroker (VM, 2026-10-21):** 1 px strokes of ovals and round rects differ by up to 50 levels at joins and arcs; the Java stroker outline filled by Skia measured best (rrect ≤ 37, oval 33) at one stroker pass per draw; Skia's path stroke worse. Decision open: keep analytic strokes (fast, ≤ 50 levels at 2x) or route strokes through `createStrokedShape`.
- **`sun.java2d.skia.lcd=accumulate`:** fastest and most faithful LCD mode on Dawn (VM, 2026-10-20), not the default (user). Before it can be: the scratch is sized to the largest LCD destination the context ever had and never released (59 MB at 5K), and the composite is two bounding-box fills per batch per colour (review round 5 F6); a bounds-sized scratch with idle release, or a cap with fallback to two-pass. Measurement on real Windows hardware with a discrete GPU is the VM's next item.
- **Windows (hand-over round 5):** (1) `LcdTextBench` with the two-pass **overlap split** (Mac fix 2026-10-19, §11.20) in the default `twopass`; if the draw count makes it slower than the software loops, implement the split on ink overlap (per-cell ink bounds above a coverage threshold, computed at upload) and measure again; (2) the LCD two-pass shader evaluates two `pow` per channel per pass: a per-colour LUT or a cheaper fit would move it toward the grayscale cost; (3) re-run `LcdBackgrounds` with an overlapping second string (the Mac version of the probe draws one) in both modes.

## 14. Design decisions to re-evaluate (cross-backend, potentially costly)

Decisions that were right for the prototype but trade something away; each should be re-measured when the measurement in the last column is available, and compared with how Metal/Vulkan/software do it.

| # | Decision (date, §) | What it costs or risks | Alternative(s) | Re-evaluate when / how |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **All non-AA geometry is meshes**, never `drawRect`/`drawPath`/`drawImageRect` (2026-10-14, §10, §11.13) | Bypasses Graphite's analytic renderers and any batching Graphite does for rects/images; one `drawVertices` per blit or parallelogram (~2 µs CPU each, same as any Graphite draw). Fractional UI scales (125/150 % on Windows) do not change the cost — the rule applies at 100 % too; they change what would happen *without* it: at fractional scales nothing is pixel-aligned in device space, so Graphite's own primitives would anti-alias every non-AA rect, line and image (blur, not a slowdown). | (a) Graphite `drawEdgeAAQuad`/`drawEdgeAAImageSet` with `aaFlags = kNone` (per-edge AA control, experimental API); (b) accept Graphite's AA on non-AA primitives (visual change product-wide, what Skiko-based UIs live with); (c) upstream feature request for honouring non-AA on paths/images. | RenderPerfTest `Image*`/`FlatBox`/`ClipRotated*` at 100 % vs 150 % `uiScale` on Skia vs Metal; IDE scenes at 125/150 % on Windows (WP 2.2) — blits and rect-heavy scenes are where the mesh path dominates. **Measured 2026-10-14 (§11.7b): no scale-dependent cost, 0.95× at 1.0 vs 0.94× at 1.5.** Alternative (a) measured (§11.14): same speed, pixels not identical, experimental API — knob `sun.java2d.skia.blitQuads=edgeAA` kept for Windows/Dawn. |
| D2 | **Graphite internal MSAA off** (`fInternalMultisampleCount = k1`) (2026-10-14, §10) | AA paths go through Graphite's path atlas (CPU raster atlas or compute atlas) instead of the MSAA stencil/cover renderers: more CPU per AA path, atlas texture memory and uploads. Only AA parallelograms use it in variant 1; **variant 2 (Skia rasterizing rrects/paths) and SVG icons would be fully exposed.** Chrome runs Graphite with MSAA on; we deviate from the configuration Skia tests most. Upside: no multisampled render targets (bandwidth/memory, relevant on iGPUs and tiles), and 8-bit coverage instead of 4 levels. | (a) MSAA on + render non-AA geometry in separate passes (flush around it — kills batching); (b) MSAA on + accept 4-level edges on non-AA geometry drawn in the same pass as a path (what the matrix caught as 10 suspects); (c) `fInternalMSAATileSize`/per-draw thresholds once Skia exposes them. | RenderPerfTest AA tests and `test/jdk/performance` path-heavy scenes with `k1` vs `k4`; variant 2 measurements must be done both ways; power on a laptop. Note that rrects/ovals/rects use the analytic renderer either way — only general paths differ. **Measured 2026-10-14 (§11.7b, `sun.java2d.skia.msaa=4` knob, `91b543896954`): AA parallelograms 0.75–0.85×, everything else ≥ 1.0×; geomean 0.97× over 8 AA tests.** Variant 2 and SVG icons still open. |
| D3 | **Marlin tiles via a 2048² A8 atlas + per-row staging** (§11.11) and **shader-painted tiles drawn one by one** (no `sk_FragCoord` in public ES2 SkSL) | Gradient/texture-painted AA shapes cost one draw per 32×32 tile (an islands header at 1000×40 ≈ 64 draws per repaint). | Private `SkRuntimeEffectPriv` access for `sk_FragCoord`; a mesh with per-vertex canvas coordinates as a second attribute (not expressible in `SkVertices` without custom attributes → `SkMesh`, which Graphite m156 does not support); variant 2 (Skia rasterizes the shape, no tiles). | IDE scene measurements (gradient headers, islands) — count tile draws per frame with `J2D_TRACE_LEVEL`. |
| D4 | **XOR as a runtime blender with bit-by-bit arithmetic** (§11.12) | A ~30-iteration loop per pixel in the fragment shader; `RenderPerfTest *_XOR` tests are excluded from the geomean so the cost is unmeasured. Metal's XOR differs from software anyway, so "match software exactly" was a choice. | Integer SkSL (needs non-ES2 runtime effects, not available for blenders in m156); match Metal's cheaper semantics instead of software's. | Run the XOR benchmark subset; decide which backend's XOR is the reference before investing. |
| D5 | **Global native render state reconciled by Java-side invalidation** (§13) | Correct as long as every context switch invalidates; multi-display and multi-context scenes are untested. | Per-`SKConfigContext` state as Metal does. | Before multi-display measurements. |
| D6 | **Glyph atlas from `AccelGlyphCache` + our own mesh batching** instead of Skia's text stack (§11.9) | Reimplements what Skia's glyph atlas does, but keeps Java's rasterizer, metrics and LCD semantics (the whole point of variant 1 / path b). | Skia text (path a, WP 3.2). | WP 3.2. |
| D7 | **Shape-mode and MSAA defaults are the same on every GPU API** (`analytic`, `msaa=1`; 2026-10-16, §10) | On Dawn/D3D11 `msaa=4` would make general paths and AA parallelograms display-rate (`FlatQuadAA` 13 → 120, `RotatedBoxAA` 40 → 120) where Metal gains little; the uniform default leaves that on the table. | Per-API defaults table (`SKEnvironment` already has the architecture-dependent configuration hook). | When Windows runs on real hardware: measure `msaa=4` coverage quantisation (§11.7d) against the general-path share of IDE scenes. |
| D8 | **LCD glyph batches are per colour** (the LCD blender takes the text colour as a uniform; per-channel coverage in `src.rgb`) (2026-10-16, §11.19) | `TextLCD` 0.1–0.2× GDI on Dawn, ~6 ms CPU per frame for 63k glyphs; the Metal pipeline draws LCD glyphs one by one (~155 ms for 1,500 distinct glyphs) | Colour in a second vertex attribute consumed by the blender, or dropping the LCD mode (Laurent's JBR-7462 TODO; macOS has had no subpixel text since 10.14) | When IDE LCD usage is measured; LWCToolkit still publishes LCD_HRGB as the desktop hint. |
| D9 | **LCD text without a destination read is two fixed-function passes with the gamma folded into the coverage** (`sun.java2d.skia.lcd=twopass`, Windows default; 2026-10-19, §10, §11.20) | ≤ 15 levels off at glyph edges for light text on mid-tones (exact on white and black); two draws per batch; with the overlap split, one draw pair per overlapping group (≈ per glyph on Dawn, unmeasured); two `pow` per channel per pass. | `blend` (exact, needs framebuffer fetch to be cheap); grayscale AA for LCD requests; Skia's own LCD text with dual-source blending (bypasses Java's glyph images); a Skia change exposing dual-source output to runtime effects. | `LcdTextBench` on Dawn with the split; IDE dark-theme scenes on Windows; a Vulkan measurement before `twopass` stays its default. |

## 15. Candidates for upstream reports or questions (Skia)

Checked against Skia's own documentation before listing; "report?" is my current judgement.

| # | Observation | Where seen | Report? |
|---|---|---|---|
| U1 | **`SkSurfaces::AsImage` ordering is the client's job** — a `writePixels` recorded after a draw that samples the atlas may or may not be applied before that draw, depending on device creation order (SrcMaskOps). Surface.h says so explicitly: *"care must be taken by the client to ensure that the contents of the image reflect the desired contents when it is consumed by the gpu."* | §11.13 rule 3 | **No bug.** At most a doc request: spell out that *record order* is not the guarantee and that a sampling draw (or a snap) is what pins an upload. Our fix (upload before every mesh draw) is the intended usage. |
| U2 | **Non-AA is honoured only for axis-aligned rects**; rotated/sheared rects, paths and image rects are always anti-aliased (`Device::drawRect` snaps, nothing else does). Documented only in a source comment ("Graphite assumes everything is anti-aliased"). | §11.13 rule 1 | **Feature request / question** worth asking: is per-edge AA via `drawEdgeAAQuad`/`drawEdgeAAImageSet` the intended way to get non-AA geometry in Graphite, and will it stay public? A pixel-exact 2D API on top of Graphite (ours, Flutter's non-AA modes) needs an answer. |
| U3 | **Whole-pass MSAA**: one MSAA draw multisamples every draw in the pass, so non-AA edges recorded in the same frame get 4-level coverage. | §11.13 rule 2 | Design, not a bug; could be raised together with U2 (the two interact: without U2 nothing would need U3). |
| U4 | **`drawAtlas` becomes one draw + one paint key per sprite** in Graphite (profiled; ~2 µs each), unlike Ganesh. | §11.9 | **Worth a performance report** with the sample profile; the API doc still describes it as a batching primitive. |
| U5 | **`sk_FragCoord` unavailable to public runtime effects in ES2 mode**; needs `SkRuntimeEffectPriv`. | §11.11, D3 | Known limitation; a question on the mailing list about plans, not a bug. |
| U6 | **`SkContexts::MakeGraphite` / `SkContext` non-functional in m156** (only `priv()` public). | §13 | Re-check on the next milestone before reporting; probably in-progress API. |
| U7 | Runtime **blenders are ES2-only SkSL** (no integer ops) even on Metal. | §11.12 | Known; same as U5. |
